There are many pensioners in Austria – more than 300 000, 400 000 – who have to live with a minimum pension, which is not necessarily to be described as social. This gross mis-evaluation and under-evaluation mainly affects women in our society, as family work is mainly done by women – mothers – in the case of childcare. In the case of internal family care, it is also true that women – as daughters or daughters-in-law – often perform these benefits, and we must not leave them in the rain! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We should finally appreciate this family work and do everything politically, so that this achievement is also honoured. In 2001, the German Federal Statistical Office calculated the value of the unpaid services provided in private households at least €820 billion or almost 40 percent of the gross domestic product. To our country and the year 2008 these unpaid services of Austrian households would represent a value of about € 110 billion, which is a huge value. However, these benefits are not honoured. Every traffic jam where the engines run pointlessly and fuel is burned does not increase gross domestic product, but family work of over €100 billion does not increase. There is an urgent need to think about this in our society. There must be appropriate recognitions to ensure that the achievements of these people are also honoured, otherwise they will be discontinued for a short or a long time – and that would be a disaster for our society and for our family policy development! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] The increase in childlessness in our society is a first dramatic sign – and not only since today: For over three decades it has been going downhill, the last positive, strong birth year was 1969! Where have the responsible politicians been in the last decades, who should have thought about how this dramatic childlessness is happening in our society and how can we counter this dramatic demographic development? How can we strengthen and support our Austrian families so that they can afford more children and bring children and families back to work? – We need to think about it because children are our future. We hear this sentence very often in these “sacred halls, ” but only to pronounce it and to use it as a floscle is too little; something has to happen quickly. We need a change of family policy in our society, towards a family-oriented policy, towards a family-oriented economic policy, and not to an economic-oriented family policy! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] That's exactly what we're experiencing with this novel. However, we must not forget, as happens in our society in part, those who want to stay with their children for a longer period of time. There, I think, must be put on, because the earlier the return to the profession takes place, the lower the probability of the birth of a second or third child. And the fact is, we're just experiencing that. Austria is already that country in Europe in which – in addition to Italy – the child wish is the least. We also have the highest share of childrenless women in our society. The reasons for this are, of course, also in the desire for a career, in economic thinking and in limited possibilities in our society. These developments are not indifferent to us, because without children, there is no future for our society and our state, and there can be no maintenance of the generation contract, which is purely mathematical. If there are fewer and fewer children in our society, it will no longer be possible to maintain this generation contract in an elderly society – and there we bear responsibility. That means we have to counter-tax and set more family-political accents. That is why we are also working for a family tax division, for an educational content for Austrian families, for a preference of parents on the labour market and for a fair pension assessment of child rearing times. This leads to gaps in the budget, particularly in the case of low-income families. This could be prevented by standardising the payment arrangements and by establishing a payment day. Point Report of the Tourism Committee on the report of the Federal Minister for Economic Affairs, Family and Youth on the situation of the tourism and leisure industry in Austria 2008 (III-95/368 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e13558	We're now on the 4th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Fixed speaking time: 4 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e13585	Mr President. High house! Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I thank you for this detailed tourism report with you, Mrs. State Secretary, and above all with the staff and our super-sector, Dr. Udolf-Strobl. 2008 was a pleasant year of tourism: as many guests as never before, 126.7 million stays, 4.5 percent more, 42 million of them in Tyrol alone. There were 181 000 self-employed workers and a contribution of 8.2 to GDP, with the leisure industry even 16 percent. For example, the cable car industry as the key economy of holiday and winter tourism generates €370 for every Austrian, i.e. about one million old Austrian schilling for the members of this Parliament, and we proudly also deliver 640 million in VAT to the budget. Each work place at the cable cars is six more in the region. Today, legendary 550 million € were invested in the cable car industry, by 5 percent more than last year. Tourism, which can be said with fug and right, is the economic form with the greatest wealth distribution, even in the remotest regions of our country. Also, the initially feared invasion of local tourism in 2009 is not included. – not in winter, the strongest in turnover, not last summer; in Tyrol we even had increases in some valleys here. Austrian tourism is well hit by the crisis. This is primarily due to the great, internationally well-positioned companies, the diligent employees and motivated businessmen and entrepreneurs. The increased advertising budgets in Austria and in the countries have also led to unexpected success. I express my thanks to our Minister Mitterlehner for this. However, the city-hotel industry had to lose 6 percent in Vienna, and concern is still the situation in the national parks, where there are sometimes strong declines in nightfalls, for the past 30 years. It is not enough to maintain only the ecologists and the farmers at a high level, where product maintenance and marketing must become part and task of the National Park administrations. One can only continue to inspire the local population in the parks if they are economically successful. Here I would like to thank you, Mr Minister - Mrs State Secretary - for setting up the Task Force, a platform on which this problem is handled highly professionally in your home. Austria has thus gained international market shares. Advertising efforts in the domestic market and also in the local markets have paid off. The budgets of Austria's advertising with around 50 million are supplemented by the LTOs and the funds of the countries to approximately 100 million. We are thus internationally comparable. It is true, however, that the coordination and use of these funds between organisations must be better coordinated. The major associations in the West belong to the common and coordinated applications of the markets. These tourism associations have more respites than some federal states, such as Burgenland, such as Lower Austria, like Upper Austria, and therefore belong to the necessary coordinations on the market. Here, too, you are very active, Mr Minister, Secretary of State – thank you very much here too! The European Capital of Culture Linz 2009 has certainly had a positive impact on tourism. It is also gratifying to see the increase in funding applications for the Austrian Hotel Trustee Hand. Here, under Dr. Mücke and Dr. Hartl, excellent work and most professional advice is done. The restructuring fund, a restructuring programme for in need, has hardly increased. The much quoted banking crisis has hardly arrived in tourism. I've been an innkeeper for 32 years, in my youth I've still gotten the credit barrier in the seventies and experienced the high interest rate phase at the beginning of the eighties and later in the nineties on my own. Our banking problems today are at least relatively low in tourism. We still have the biggest problem on the labour market. Despite enormous increases in unemployment, we have not succeeded in motivating the unemployed from the industry to accept jobs in tourism. Unfortunately, service must also be provided at unattractive daily and weekly times. This problem, namely the acceptance of jobs in service, cannot solve tourism alone. Businesses in tourism must pay for market-compliant wages, decent working conditions and reasonable accommodation worry. They do. The adoption of open jobs is a social challenge that we must face together. In addition to solving the staff problem, we would like to see reliefs in the business system and in the environmental bureaucracy. We also need help with the unfortunately dwindling student lessons. There's a lot going on here, in the federal states, for example, free rides at the cable cars. This should be coordinated, and we need a coordinator here. Mr Redl, who has done good work here in the Ministry of Education, retired and is not re-occupied. The 70 percent approval clause, whether or not you are on a school-ship course, is also to be questioned. All in all, we are proud of our businesses and the diligent employees. How does Wolfgang Schüssel always say so well?: Tourism is hard work with a friendly and charming smile on the lips. Thank you.

The creation of a tourism-friendly climate in Austria is essential. Tourism is a cross-sectional matter. It is therefore also important that offers can also be used by the local population. Today, the “standard” is an article on school skiing courses. Of course, this is also about long-term customer commitment to the leisure industry, but in the end there is also the question: Can people, the local population, afford to do so? And again, the saying goes: If people are fine, the economy is fine. And that is why we should see that the people in Austria, everyone, are as good as possible.

The problem of National Park and Tourism is practically known in all EU national parks, and I think Austria could play an important role in this. With regard to the organisation, the product responsibility lies clearly with the National Park administrations as strategic inputs. With a seal of quality “official partners of the Austrian National Parks”, tourism companies could be won as partners and significantly improved their international market presence. ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e15357	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Economie auditors, business loyals, business loyalty law and audit quality assurance law – difficult titles, but also a difficult meteor. In Austria, we are lucky to have a highly developed profession of tax advisors for decades, summarized in the Chamber of Economic Trustees; tax advisors, auditors, accountants, independent accountants, all of this summarized. The profession has developed into an already almost academic profession – almost throughout – into a profession that requires a very remarkable and high level of education and training. You don't become so simple business loyals, you don't just become tax advisors. – After the corresponding studies, a practice of three years has to be completed in a relevant company, and an examination has to be carried out there, an examination that is very, very complex and requires a high quality. Then you're a tax advisor. If you want to take the next stage and become an auditor, you have to complete another three years of practice in a relevant firm. ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e17989	Mrs. Dr. Karl is now speaking. I'm willing to set the clock to 4 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e18010	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In today's debate, too, the opponents of the transfer account repeatedly argue that it is unclear what the added value of a transfer account lies in. – This view is based either on ignorance of the Austrian social and tax system or on a refusal of reality or, worst of all, on both. There are very good reasons for the introduction of a transfer account. One of these reasons has already been mentioned several times today and is called transparency. The aim is to provide both the competent authorities and the service providers with transparency as to what transfers are used to what extent. This does not mean, however, that these data must also be made public. Thus, such a transfer account does not lead to an ever-increasing debate of envy, but simply to greater transparency for the competent authorities and for the beneficiaries. [[Call the SPÖ: For whom?]] [[New continuous calls to the SPÖ.]] You know, simply pretending to be something of negligible importance to transparency is simply negligent and also hypocritical – it seems to be because transparency is very much welcomed elsewhere. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP]] A very good example of already lived transparency is the obligation to provide information in the field of health insurance. The health insurance institutions must inform the insured persons of the benefits they receive. Please, have you heard a debate of envy when this obligation of information was introduced? Has the introduction of this information obligation led to a division of the Solidarity in Health Insurance? Has this introduction led to a reduction in health care? – All these questions have to be answered with a clear no. Another advantage of the transfer account is that it shows counterproductive threshold phenomena. For many people in employment, the benefit is no longer worth it, because social transfers suddenly disappear, but the tax progression is fully committed. However, work must pay off; it has already been said several times today. If work is no longer worth it, then there is an urgent need for action. I therefore believe that it is an urgent task not to occupy services negatively, but rather to create incentives for performance. Klubobmann Bucher has already mentioned that we need a performance-enhancing tax system. However, we also need a social system that promotes performance. Unfortunately, in our social system, there are a number of performance inhibitors. This includes, for example, the so-called all-or-non-principle. This means that a social benefit is completely lost if you earn only one cent above a certain income limit. In the area of social security, this is usually the low-quality limit. If you earn only one cent over it, the entire performance will be lost. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Silhavy.]] For example, think of an unemployment benefit worker who receives 1 000 € unemployment benefit, who receives part-time employment of 500 €, and if he accepts this job, he loses 1 000 €. That means, for the person concerned, €500 is worth losing €1,000 in social benefits. Is that a positive incentive for performance? I do not think so. We must seek and eliminate such negative incentives. A transfer account is also to be welcomed because it opens the way to coordinate transfers made by different bodies at federal, regional and local level. This can also increase the reliability of the meeting. It was also mentioned by the Chancellor that the poorest of the poor and the workers should not pay the bill of the crisis. We don't want that either! What we want is that those who need the services also get these services because they are equipped with more reliability. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In conclusion, it is clear what the benefits of the transfer account are: Increased transparency, removal of performance barriers and increased performance reliability. [[New call of the abg. Silhavy.]] All these are advantages to which one should not say only No from the outset, but which one should very well discuss. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e20937	Mrs. Federal Minister Dr. Schmied is now speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e20961	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. High house! Ladies and gentlemen, at the beginning of my statement, I would like to talk about one thing very clearly. We discussed it in detail in the Committee on Culture, at the request of Mr Petzner: That is the question of responsibility. It is always very important for me to explain this in the first place. The responsibility for the construction, renovation and security measures in the Federal Museums is clearly regulated in the Federal Ministry Act. The Ministry of Economic Affairs is responsible for planning, commissioning and implementing necessary construction and renovation measures. In the case of the Albertina, this means the entire construction and maintenance of the storage warehouse and all measures for the installation of the storage system. So it's important for me to explain the responsibility. The Ministry of Education, Arts and Culture is responsible for the establishment of the premises and the security measures for the works of art. In the Albertina this means the equipment with warning systems and safety plans. I therefore have – and only for that reason! – in response to the question raised today, the questions answered in detail, which concern my department competence, and in those questions falling within the competence of the Ministry of Economic Affairs, referred to the answer to the request which was partially equivalent. Of course, to answer your question, there have also been votes between the two departments. This corresponds to a coordinated approach within the Federal Government. And, of course, the two ministries here are constantly in constant contact. In particular, in the case of questions concerning structural details, I referred to answers from the Ministry of Economic Affairs, as these questions fall within the sole competence of the Ministry of Economic Affairs. Tenders, commissions and executions of construction work at the Federal Museums are carried out by the Head of the castle in their sole responsibility. All construction costs are also borne by the Ministry of Economic Affairs. The Ministry of Economic Affairs shall bear all the consequences of the construction work in the context of the renovation, i.e. repair or other follow-up costs. I have therefore referred to questions of civil liability and liability and today also refer to the assessment of the Ministry of Economic Affairs. Now to that part of the questions that concern my place of business. The Albertina's approach in close coordination with the Federal Monumental Office, which, in order to safeguard the works of art, also falls into my department's competence, which were the evacuation and storage in the bastei hall – and I would like to emphasize this – exemplary and always in the sense of the safety of the works of art. The contact with Director Schröder is close and I am constantly informed about the current developments and the steps to be taken. The renovation work will be completed in the next few weeks, and Director Schröder informed the public yesterday about the positive development that the Basteihalle, currently used as a provisional depot, can be used again for exhibition purposes from the end of January 2010. Madam President, you referred to the water entry of September. I was immediately informed by Director Schröder. At that time all the artworks had already been evacuated and there was also a brief information from Director Schröder to the media, which obviously was not taken further. On the question of the causes. – The causes of water entry are clear, have also been discussed in the public. This happened in connection with the installation. The liability issues are currently being clarified. I would like to make it clear to you that, from January 2010, there will be free entry into the federal museums for young people up to 19 years old, but the basic salary has not been deducted and reduced at the federal museums, but I recall the budget decisions: The basic fee for the Federal Museums has been significantly increased. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e22345	We're coming to the 7th. Agenda item. The rapporteur is a Member of Parliament, a jewelry beater; he does not wish to report. I call Mr Spadiut. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e22373	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The amendment to the Wine Act provides for the withdrawal of the term ‘tapling wine’ in § 8. The previous table wine has only to meet basic requirements not to be corrupt. Labels were not allowed to have smaller geographical indications, annual designations or variety designations. The new scheme provides for a maximum hectares limit of 9 000 kg or 6 750 litres of wine, as well as a typical type of grape variety and vintage. – But with this, the alleged improvement to the old table wine already ends. In the future, this wine may have labels with the variety designation and/or the vintage. Thus, low-quality wines can or may look the same as high-quality products through appropriate bottles and labels. The winegrowers welcome this change. It is interesting, however, that in autumn 2008, the president of the Austrian Wine Association Josef Pleil, had little joy with this change, he said: We must make it possible that we do not carry good Austrian quality wine in favour of a branded table wine! Otherwise this sounds already in September 2009. That's what he said: We urgently need the decision of the Austrian Wine Act so that the quality level of Austrian wine does not cause any damage and neither producers nor consumers are confused by name! The reason for this change of mind is enigmatic. Perhaps the statement in the autumn of the previous year was also influenced by the forthcoming National Council elections. The new name makes it difficult for consumers to distinguish between inferior and high-quality wines. The buyer must deal with the labels in detail to find out the difference. We must fail this law, as it helps to mask the quality of Austrian wines in marketing and disadvantages existing regional quality features. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] Especially in Austria, which, after the wine scandal, has regained its name in the international wine market by intense efforts, especially for the good quality, such a section in a wine law should not have lost anything.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e22419	Next speaker: Member of the Council of Ministers of the European Communities. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e22440	Mr President. Estimated Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I may refer briefly to the Wine Law 2009. We have made adjustments due to the wine market reform of the EU decided in 2008. This reform is divided into two parts. One is the wine market-related part – which entered into force in 2008 – where we receive and disbursement from the EU through restructuring and conversion measures in the field of vineyards in order to convert the vineyards. Furthermore, these are investment measures in processing and sales, as well as in exports to third countries. The aim is to produce the highest possible quality of grapes from the vineyard and then to kiltern with state-of-the-art technology. From the gentle acquisition of grapes to a cooled fermentation to an absolutely modern top filling, we want to provide the quality spoiled Austrian wine customer with really best quality, and we are very proud that we have such an acclaim. The second part is the wine part. Unfortunately, I can't explain to my previous speaker. We have already tried to do so in committee, but we have not been able to make it clear to him there either. There is a new classification into the categories wine without origin and wine with origin. This is part of the EU's food law, because we need to declare its origin better. However, we have managed to maintain the previous quality regime of Austrian wine – that is, the quality pyramid. This is the great victory that we have succeeded. The wine without any indication of origin, which has so far been called table wine, will only be sold in the future with the name “wine”. If it has added a grape variety wine – that is, if it is expressed in the variety – it must correspond to the higher quality level as regards the highest yield of hectares and, of course, error-free sensory and type of variety. We are already in the next quality level, and that is the wine with protected geographical indication. That was the country wine so far. This designation remains valid. This wine must come to 100 percent from the named wine-growing region. The highest level in this respect is the wine with a protected designation of origin. There are the quality wines – the DAC wines – as well as the predicate wines – which contain late lees, gleans, berries harvests, outbreaks, dried berries harvests and ice wine. [[Abg. Singer: Giebelkreuzwein!]] [[Rejoicing at the FPÖ]] There are no innovations in the quality wine with regard to the designations – they are completely maintained. In other words, there is no change for the tested consumer, and there is no change in the practice of production for the producer. These are very important points. The innovation, however, is that we now have the opportunity to fill quality wine in other containers. There's always talk about the "Bag-in-Boxes". This is a very important aspect to be able to score on the export markets for Austrian quality wine, because especially in the Scandinavian countries and in the UK, these bag-in-boxes are very common. We have not yet been able to take a foothold. ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e23314	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! Maybe once again to § 8: I would also like to thank Mr Pirklhuber, who is really involved. Therefore, one can also recommend the BZÖ to go along this path. They would make the right decisions in the sense of the Austrian winegrowers and, above all, consumers! Very briefly: What's this about? – It is one of the most important points of the renewal of EU wine law. This is the departure from the categorisation of wines in quality wine and table wine. That is to say, it is now wines with a protected designation of origin that are a term for us under “quality wine” and must continue to be named in Austria, and they are wines with a protected geographical indication, which are the agricultural wines for which the same applies. It is new that the wine is now equipped with the possibility of indicating grape varieties and vintages without any further indication of its origin as “Austria” – that is, the former table wine – but is also raised at the same time to the level of the agricultural wine, i.e. a quantity restriction. There was no such thing as table wine before. The name ‘tapella wine’ is deleted, and this wine is added to the category ‘agricultural wine’, i.e. it experiences a quality upgrade. There is also wine without an indication of origin. This can only be referred to as “wine”, and this is the only way to place actual excesses. This wine hardly has a market in Austria. Only a short time. Your request, Mr Pirklhuber, concerns the mushroom-resistant varieties: I have a list of: 700 mushroom-resistant varieties are currently in the various stages of the control test. No decision has yet been taken which would be the most ideal to include them in the quality rebsort register in Austria as well. And that's exactly what it's all about. I am in favour – and here I am full of you – of the fact that in future we will also need such varieties as quality in Austria, but of course we need the necessary recognition and control before. And when this is complete and in fact the most ideal varieties – I say now – are selected, it will also be possible to include these varieties in the quality reb variety register. Of course, the Federal Minister can do so by means of a regulation. Thus, even the organic farmers will be in line if all tests are actually completed. In this sense, I would like to invite all of you to agree to this wine law, as has been said, in the sense of Austrian quality wine production. One thing winegrowers were always clear to us: We don't need any more wine, but we also need really good prices for our high quality wines! Thank you.

Point Report of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection on the Government template (318 e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law on the Monitoring of Trade in Examples of Wildlife and Plant Species (Artenhandelsgesetz 2009 – ArtHG 2009) (348 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e24303	We shall now proceed to item 8 of the agenda. There was no oral reporting. Mr Ing. is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats. Schools. Fixed speaking time: 4 minutes.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e24331	Mr President. Estimated Minister of State! Dear ladies and gentlemen in the High House! After the wine law has broken out the dramatic possibilities of this house, we now come to a subject that may provide more rest. We are reforming the Art Trade Act. It is about adapting to the EU Regulation on the protection of species in a number of respects and, at the same time, implementing some simplifications within Austria. The purpose of this law is to protect species protected by international agreements which are endangered in their stock and to restrict or prohibit trade or the behaviour of such species in Austria. Last year, it was the case that there were around a hundred cases that led to a complaint, a hundred cases that were partly dealt with by customs, or within Austria by the District Headquarters. It is not a high number of offences, but a number that have led to complicated administrative and criminal proceedings. And so we have seen that, in the course of this reform, it would also be beneficial to put the procedure together and simply explain the customs duty in the future. This means that in the future these procedures will in principle be conducted as a financial procedure. The administrative simplification will be a very rapid one, while at the same time ensuring that both at the border on entry and in domestic checks these issues will be treated equally and by officials trained in accordance with the same training standards. At the same time, it should be assumed that the customs officials can also deal with the cases, so to speak, and that the penalties can also be imposed, in about 50 percent of the cases in travel, and can be dealt with immediately. so that the people who have committed a violation also know how they are, and the story is done. We're here on the topic of species conservation. Species protection is a question that is more likely to have to do with man's overall responsibility for creation. Some people have not yet recognized these issues. I believe that it is important for us and for the planet and hence for each of us to assume our responsibility for the protection of endangered species. [[Congratulations on MEPs of the ÖVP.]] It's a subject that's important to us. This is not only about protecting individual species from extinction, but also about protecting the habitats of various animals, birds, and fish, in such a way that they can continue to fulfil their habitat function. What does that mean? This means that we have to take the issues of climate change seriously, that we have to take matters of pressure on use seriously, that are fisheries issues, many comprehensive issues that cannot be solved with a purely import limitation on protected species. This law is a small part of our overall responsibility, but that also needs to be done properly. And the new possibilities give us the certainty that on the one hand it will be cost-effective but on the other hand efficient, just as we expect from this government. I thank Federal Minister Berlakovich for bringing us this presentation.

I would also like to note that very often live animals are smuggled into commercial areas, where parrots, for example, are placed in paper rolls, stacked in suitcases and transported through the half-world without food and water. This causes about 90 percent of live smuggled animals to end up in pain during transport. This also has the effect that, for example, in the Amazon region alone, approximately $20 billion of sales are made with the trade and smuggling of animals and plants that are on red lists and whose trade is prohibited. We also know that these animals, if they still live, or the 10 percent that survive, then land in laboratories or private zoos. Some are also processed into potency agents or any status symbols. And even with plants, there is the very concrete problem that more and more so-called aliens are also being smuggled into Austria – also via the Internet – which then leads to difficulties, in part, to genetic difficulties in meeting native species. Of course, the question of globalisation plays a major role in the tourism infringement of the Art Trade Act, but very often also the lack of awareness of injustice. be the ignorance that something is forbidden. I think it would be very useful to work much closer and much more intensive with travel providers, with travel agencies, to inform tourists about the existing bans. There have been two species-protection dogs named Amy and Uno at Frankfurt Airport for a year now, which are helping sniffly to detect species-protection violations. And when used in Frankfurt, for example, they used a bear skull, a stuffed puma, banned caviar, ivory figures, products made of snake leather, shark fins, primate skull and other perverts. I am convinced that we should also take advantage of the experience of German colleagues and use animal support, animal reinforcement at customs and join the dogs in the fight for biodiversity and species protection. By the way, I am pleased to see the unanimous adoption of this novel.

(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) As has already been said by my previous speakers, the aim of this law on trade in species, which is being adopted in the plenary session, is to eliminate the existing twofolds and ambiguities within national enforcement. The evaluation of the experience so far shows that ad has been reimbursed mainly by customs authorities. In the future, therefore, implementation by law is bundled exactly where the highest catch-up results are already achieved today, i.e. with customs and financial criminal authorities. Therefore, export and import controls, domestic controls and the management of investigation procedures and administrative criminal proceedings will be concentrated in an authority and thus the efficiency of the administration will be increased. Further improvements to the status quo: Point one: The possibility to impose simplified penalties on minor offences will undoubtedly lead to a sustainable learning effect on smugglers. Point two: Increase in efficiency within the authority. Point three: Taking into account the latest EU targets. This law therefore actively contributes to improving quality and efficiency within Austrian enforcement. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e25181	Next speaker: Mr Steier. Also 2 minutes' speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-10-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00041_d8e25203	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Souvenirs and cubs made of ivory, reptile leather or tropical wood are often brought home by almost every holidaymaker. Tourists usually do not always know that these products are subject to strict import regulations under the Washington Convention on Species. This Convention protects 3,000 species of animals and 30 000 species of plants threatened by international trade. In Austria, 1 580 attacks were carried out in 2008 in accordance with the species protection regulations. For these reasons, we are deciding today on this novel to the Art Trade Act. It will bring about improvements in the monitoring of trade in wild animal and plant species, inter alia, through the concentration of execution on customs. Ladies and gentlemen, allow me a few words on the Internet. Thanks to modern technology, it is relatively easy to also buy products made from endangered species via the network. Of course, there is also a distinction between those buyers who consciously use the advantage of high anonymity when buying illegal specimens, and those who unwittingly acquire species-protected products. This market is partly not adequately controlled and it will require much observation and action at all relevant political levels in order to stop these efforts. Let's do it together and protect our species and wildlife! - Thank you.

If we look at the range of legal minimum wages within the EU, we see that this goes from €9.73 per hour in Luxembourg to €0.71 in Romania. That is, legal minimum wages do not guarantee that you have a real life-saving income. Today's discussion on performance justice shows me much more clearly that it is far better to leave wage and collective treaty negotiations to the social partners. [[Abg. Dolinschek: But everyone has none!]] After all, what is justice of performance? Is performance justice something that can be measured in labour production? Is the number of items you get in a factory worth more than the care of a family?, I ask you. How do you rate the performance in the school? – In there are performance assessments? By what standards of goodness do you do that? – There are different perceptions of performance, one can go up to the physical performance which is defined. That's exactly what's going on: If we only define what can be achieved through a labour income, then exactly those people who perform a lot of work are required by national and social policy, but who do not obtain income from it, are always called by you as those people who do not correspond to performance justice and who are not service providers. In Austria, we are fortunate to have a system that negotiates the collective agreements, minimum wages on the social partners, not only minimum wages, but also other components of wages, such as the 13th and 14ths. If you do not know, this is not a legal rule, Mrs Colleague, but also other components of wages such as supplements and the like. For those who are not covered by the collective contract scheme – many are fortunately not – there are minimum wage rates. These are also covered by social partnership. I believe that we are taking a far better step with the demand for 1 300 € minimum wage at collective contract level, because at least then it is not paid twelve times, but fourteen times. In view of the wage negotiations, I call on the representatives of the economy to also remember that it is essential to achieve better minimum wages at the social security level, namely € 1 300. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Finally, let me say a sentence: The most important of all, namely those with precarious employment conditions, does not help you with this request. I believe that this is an issue that we must deal with in particular, because we have a lot of industries in which we are getting fewer and less normal working conditions and more and more precarious working conditions.
